{{ar:يُوسُفُ}} ({{tr:Yūsufu}})'s brother, not an independently introduced figure. Its five-nouns nominative form is grammatically marked and lets the kinship relation share the subject role with Yusuf. That matters for the argument: the brothers are not just complaining about one beloved child, but about a compact dyad set against {{ar:نَحْنُ}} ({{tr:naḥnu}}) and {{ar:عُصْبَةٌ}} ({{tr:ʿuṣbatun}}). Its glottal opening and long ū make the added kinship word audible after the clipped connector, while the boundary from 12:7 narrows the broad brothers-frame into this one dyadic relation. Later brother-language reverses the force of this label, especially when Yusuf discloses the bond to his brother in 12:69 and the brothers weaponize sibling association again in 12:77.\",\"root_display\":\"{{ar:أ خ و}} ({{tr:ʾ-kh-w}})\",\"root_gloss_range\":\"brotherhood, kinship, and shared-category relation; here the possessed singular narrows the field to Yusuf's specific brother\",\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:أَخُوهُ}} ({{tr:akhūhu}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"12:8:19:accusation-formula","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_3e54ce78b6ca9e91ff65","text":"{\"blocking_evidence\":null,\"headline\":\"public polemic enters family speech\",\"reader_payoff\":\"The reader hears the family complaint borrowing the register of public manifest-error accusations, including prophet-rejection language at 7:60.\",\"reason\":\"The rows tie the phrase to cross-surah accusation formulas and to irony in applying clarity language to the father.\",\"representative_source_ids\":[\"MI-6b8c148a\",\"QE-e6c39847\",\"MI-e1ea8778\"],\"status\":\"used\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"12:8:10","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_3ea5b5ad94fa4764fed8","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"comparative preposition plus first-person plural suffix, marking the speaking group as lower comparatum\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:مِنَّا}} ({{tr:minnā}}) closes the comparison by making the brothers the lower comparatum: the dyad is more beloved than us. The root's separation and distinction image fits their sense that right judgment is plainly apart from the father's preference, yet local agreement keeps the word as an adjective for the error noun. Surah 12 later reworks clarity-language in different moral settings: Satan is an obvious enemy in 12:5, public accusation recurs at 12:30, and recognition arrives in 12:100. genitive gerund naming an open-ended condition of straying or misjudgment\",\"prose\":\"{{ar:ضَلَالٍ}} ({{tr:ḍalālin}}) is the core of the accusation. The lost-path image remains active, but locally it is narrowed to cognitive and moral misjudgment about paternal preference. Its indefiniteness leaves the error broad, while the following adjective tightens it into a claimed manifest condition. The phrase also participates in a wider accusation formula: the same kind of charge returns in Surah 12 against other figures and back onto the accusers (12:30; 12:95; 12:97), and it echoes broader polemical uses such as 7:60 and 26:97. The heavy sound and paired ending help make the brothers' verdict feel grave, even though it is their human judgment.\",\"root_display\":\"{{ar:ض ل ل}} ({{tr:ḍ-l-l}})\",\"root_gloss_range\":\"straying, being lost, error, and disappearance from the right way; here the gerund names a state of misjudgment rather than an external misleader\",\"surface_display\":\"{{ar:ضَلَالٍ}} ({{tr:ḍalālin}})\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"12:8:13:bound-group-identity","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_b6f236465df51030b7bf","text":"{\"blocking_evidence\":null,\"headline\":\"collective singular packages many as one band\",\"reader_payoff\":\"The reader sees the brothers claiming not just number but a single consolidated social body.\",\"reason\":\"The nominative collective singular predicates group identity of the explicit pronoun.\",\"representative_source_ids\":[\"QG-593563cb\",\"QG-cec57b0d\",\"QF-4635081a\"],\"status\":\"used\"}","trust":"trusted"},{"branch_refs":[],"citable":true,"role":"candidate_evidence","scope":"micro","source_local_id":"12:8:3","source_type":"word_analysis","support_id":"sup_bd75a74bc8a19198708e","text":"{\"gloss_range\":\"fronted emphatic particle attached to Yusuf's name at the first assertion peak\",\"prose\":\"The first {{ar:لَ}} ({{tr:la}}) fuses emphasis directly to {{ar:يُوسُفُ}} ({{tr:Yūsufu}}), so Yusuf's name is loaded before the predicate of love is even heard.
